# Source 303: elitescience

Reviewed 2026-10-25, Europe/Warsaw. [Source](https://elitereads.com/genre/science-technology). New canonical source; tracking query removed.

## Scope and method

The user explicitly requested reuse of existing decisions and detailed assessment only for new books. Existing gates, attainment, deductions and R&D are retained. Only distinct source coverage changes REL. [Extraction manifest](../data/shelves_261025/inputs.json); [source/title inventory](../data/shelves_261025/candidates.json); [identity matches](../data/shelves_261025/matches.json). This intake is in progress: new titles remain in [the pending queue](../data/shelves_261025/pending.json). No completeness claim for their assessment.

All 720 list entries are present in HTML; 717 distinct title/author pairs after duplicate removal.

<a id="shelves-kindredsykes"></a>

### New: Kindred: Neanderthal Life, Love, Death and Art — Rebecca Wragg Sykes

Selected: paleoanthropologist David Frayer and archaeologist Ken Hamilton strongly recommend it, while identifying factual details, uncertain reconstructions and awkward external references. Goodreads 4.04/4,072 supports reader praise, with complaints about dense tool descriptions. This is an explanatory synthesis for adults, not specialist training; brief imagined openings do not make the predominantly nonfiction book fiction. Art is one source of archaeological evidence, not art criticism. Resource use and technology are important, but prehistoric life is the central historical period: approximate endpoint 40,000 years ago gives capped H13=-12. No serious foundational veto established from the inspected reviews.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Low priority; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 16.00 / 20 | 12.80 | Broad archaeological evidence underlies the synthesis; sparse evidence, conjecture and specific errors identified by Frayer prevent higher attainment. |
| S | 12.00 / 17 | 8.47 | Connects tools, materials, mobility, food acquisition, social behavior and genetic evidence. |
| U | 8.00 / 15 | 8.00 | Useful examples of reconstructing behavior from incomplete physical evidence, with limited direct modern strategy. |
| M | 8.00 / 12 | 8.00 | Explains constraints on survival and production but cannot firmly establish many social causes. |
| D | 8.00 / 10 | 6.40 | D=8, moderate confidence from Frayer, Hamilton and reader reports. Distinct sites and tool practices add evidence; dense lithic passages can slow the narrative but are not automatically padding. |
| R | 8.00 / 9 | 8.00 | Physical evidence and the history of interpreting it retain value; particular reconstructions remain revisable. |
| F | 4.00 / 7 | 4.00 | Technology and resource use partly fit; most content concerns prehistoric populations. |
| A | 4.50 / 5 | 4.50 | Specialist author with favorable substantive peer reviews. |
| N | 3.00 / 3 | 3.00 | Thematic organization and vivid openings improve access, though some readers find it academic. |
| P | 1.50 / 2 | 1.50 | Evocative prose is generally clear; imaginative passages must be distinguished from findings. |

<a id="shelves-flyingblind"></a>

### New: Flying Blind: The 737 MAX Tragedy and the Fall of Boeing — Peter Robison

Selected for favorable reviews by technology historian Jon Gertner and financial journalist Roger Lowenstein, and Goodreads 4.17/4,503. Industrial governance and engineering reliability are central, with worldwide aviation consequences. American corporate setting and financial incentives do not make this primarily American-tragedy rhetoric or a finance book. Gertner's criticism of the MCAS explanation and David Gelles's criticism of length constrain the assessment. No serious veto established from these reviews; not treated as current technical safety guidance.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Selective; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 16.00 / 20 | 12.80 | Reported corporate history supported by interviews and records, with limits of retrospective attribution. |
| S | 14.00 / 17 | 11.53 | Connects engineering, management incentives, production pressures and regulatory oversight. |
| U | 12.00 / 15 | 12.00 | Transferable lessons about preserving technical authority and safety feedback. |
| M | 9.00 / 12 | 9.00 | Explains organizational failure; technical MCAS account is less clear according to Gertner. |
| D | 7.00 / 10 | 4.90 | D=7, moderate confidence from Gertner, Lowenstein and Gelles reviews. Corporate decisions add mechanisms, but extended corporate drama reduces yield. No full-text audit. |
| R | 8.00 / 9 | 8.00 | Organizational reliability lessons outlast the particular crisis. |
| F | 7.00 / 7 | 7.00 | Direct engineering, production quality and industrial governance fit. |
| A | 4.50 / 5 | 4.50 | Experienced investigative business journalist with strong informed reception. |
| N | 2.50 / 3 | 2.50 | Readable institutional narrative with technical explanation weaknesses. |
| P | 1.50 / 2 | 1.50 | Clear reporting with some dramatic framing. |

### New: Mama's Last Hug: Animal Emotions and What They Tell Us about Ourselves — Frans de Waal

Selected for the favorable biologist review in The Inquisitive Biologist, corroborating critical reception, and Goodreads 4.21/5,844. Animal behavior and comparative cognition, not environmental advocacy. Observed emotional behavior is distinguished from conjecture about subjective feeling. Tom Chivers's reservations about some interpretations limit confidence, but the inspected criticism does not establish high-confidence foundational failure under V9. No health, spirituality or parenting advice veto.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Low priority; confidence moderate.

<a id="shelves-sevensinsmemory"></a>

### New: The Seven Sins of Memory: How the Mind Forgets and Remembers — Daniel L. Schacter

Selected for Alan A. Stone's enthusiastic American Journal of Psychiatry review, APA recognition, and substantial positive reader reception (Goodreads 3.88/1,900; corroborating detailed reader appraisal). This is research explanation, not primarily memory-improvement advice. Stone finds the final evolutionary interpretation weaker than the experimental exposition. That objection is retained: adaptive explanations are hypotheses, not established causes for every memory error. Inspected criticism does not justify high-confidence veto of the book's empirical framework. Broad reader praise is qualified rather than unanimous.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Selective; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 17.00 / 20 | 14.45 | Substantial experimental foundation; evolutionary explanations are less secure than the classification of errors. |
| S | 11.00 / 17 | 7.12 | Connects cognition, attention, neural evidence and real-world testimony. |
| U | 11.00 / 15 | 11.00 | Useful for evaluating recollection, source attribution and decision records. |
| M | 10.00 / 12 | 10.00 | Explains different failure mechanisms instead of treating memory as a single faculty. |
| D | 8.00 / 10 | 6.40 | D=8, moderate confidence from Stone's detailed review, published excerpt and reader reports. Distinct error classes add mechanisms; the final evolutionary synthesis is less persuasive. No full-text audit. |

<a id="shelves-evolutionsbite"></a>

### New: Evolution's Bite: A Story of Teeth, Diet, and Human Origins — Peter S. Ungar

Selected for the favorable Inquisitive Biologist review, Ann Gibbons's publisher-reproduced endorsement and positive reader reception (Goodreads 3.94/122, including substantive reports). A research narrative explaining inference from teeth, not a specialist dentistry curriculum or nutrition-advice book. The brief dietary conclusion does not determine its dominant purpose. Climate, feeding ecology and human adaptation are central, so T11 applies. Broad evolutionary timespan through modern consequences has no single historical endpoint. A creationist criticism was inspected; its appeal to the Genesis flood does not establish reasoned specialist foundational criticism under V9. No serious veto established from the inspected evidence.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Low priority; confidence moderate.
